- **Windows:** Narrow slits in barracks, wider in officer quarters, shuttered and barred
- **Decoration:** Minimal—regimental standards, unit crests, nothing frivolous
- **Condition:** Immaculate—maintenance is constant, damage repaired immediately

**Typical Street Scene - Outer Ward:**

You're walking down the main thoroughfare—it's called "The Spine." It's 40 feet wide, perfectly straight, running from outer gates to inner gates. The cobblestones are regulation-laid, forming geometric patterns.

Both sides are lined with barracks—three-story stone buildings, identical in design. Ground floor is armory and equipment storage. Second floor is sleeping quarters (bunks for 40 soldiers each). Third floor is officers' rooms and planning spaces.

Between every three barracks is a support building: a smithy (repairing weapons and armor), a quartermaster's depot (supplies stacked with military precision), a bathhouse (mandatory cleanliness), a chapel (dedicated to Thoran the Shield).

Soldiers move in small groups—never alone, always in unit formations. They salute officers automatically. They yield right-of-way to supply wagons. They don't loiter or gather in large groups.

The noise is regimented: morning bells at dawn, drilling from training yards, hammering from smithies, marching cadence from patrol changes. Even chaos is organized.

**Random Detail Table - Ironhold Streets:**
Roll d20:
1. Unit in full armor running formation drills, counting cadence
2. Weapons inspection in progress, officer examining blade quality
3. New recruits struggling through obstacle course, sergeants shouting
4. Armorer fitting custom piece for officer, taking measurements
5. Supply wagon unloading, soldiers forming chain to move goods
6. Courier running with urgent message, everyone clearing path
7. Injured soldier being carried to infirmary, comrades marching alongside
8. Target practice on archery range, arrows thudding into targets
9. Blacksmith shoeing war horses, animals stamping and snorting
10. Officer reviewing guard rotation, checking duty roster
11. Religious service in progress at chapel, somber hymns audible
12. War dogs being exercised, handlers keeping tight control
13. Engineer unit repairing wall section, working methodically
14. Intelligence officer questioning a prisoner in courtyard
15. Messenger pigeons arriving at coop, soldier logging messages
16. Commander inspecting troops, soldiers at rigid attention
17. Civilian contractor selling supplies, haggling with quartermaster
18. Healer treating minor injuries in field clinic
19. Guard change ceremony, formal and precise
20. Alarm drill—soldiers rushing to battle stations


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

### The Inner Fortress

**Approaching:**
A second set of gates—smaller but thicker—leads to the inner fortress. These gates are closed except when specifically opened. Guards here know everyone by sight. Strangers are escorted.

**Inside:**
The inner fortress is tighter, more cramped. Buildings are built directly into the mountain. Windows are minimal. Everything feels underground even when it's not.

This is where officers live, where strategy happens, where the real power resides. The air is warmer (heated by volcanic vents channeled from deep below). The light is dimmer (fewer windows, more torches).

**The Command Tower:**

The heart of Ironhold—a tower built into the mountain peak, rising 200 feet above the fortress floor. From the top, you can see 50 miles in every direction.

Inside, it's a spiral of stone stairs, war rooms on every level. Maps cover every wall. Couriers run constantly. Officers plan, debate, strategize.

The commander's office is at the very top. Sparse furnishings—a desk, chairs, weapon racks. The walls are maps and reports. The view is everything—you can see why they chose this place. Nothing approaches unseen.

**What Ironhold Offers:**
- **Security:** Safest place in the north
- **Training:** Military training for hire (expensive)
- **Intelligence:** Information about northern threats
- **Supplies:** Military-grade equipment (if you have authorization)
- **Healing:** Combat medics (field medicine, efficient but painful)

**Social Structure:**
Rank matters here more than anywhere else in Tirvandor. Military ranks are observed strictly. Civilians are tolerated but not equal. The commander's word is law.

**Why Players Might Come Here:**
- Investigating reports of giant activity
- Seeking military aid for a threat
- Learning combat skills from veterans
- Accessing intelligence reports
- Hiding from pursuit (under military protection)

---

## Crossvale - The Five Roads Hub

**Population:** ~18,000 (65% human, 20% halfling, 10% dwarf, 5% other)

### Approaching from Any Direction

**3 Miles Out:**
You see Crossvale before you reach it—not the buildings, but the roads. Five major trade routes converge here, and from a distance, they look like spokes on a wheel, all pointing to one center.

The roads are impressive—30 feet wide, paved with fitted stone, with drainage ditches on both sides. Milestones every quarter-mile. Waystations every five miles. This is serious infrastructure, maintained by serious money.

Traffic increases steadily. You're joining a river of commerce—merchant wagons, caravan trains, mounted messengers, traveling merchants with pack mules, peasants driving livestock to market.

**1 Mile Out:**
The walls come into view. Crossvale is FORTIFIED—30-foot walls of gray stone, 15 feet thick at the base. Five gates—one for each road—with fortified gatehouses, flanking towers, murder holes, the works.

This is a city that expects trouble. During the War of Three Kingdoms (1180-1186), Crossvale changed hands four times. Everyone wanted to control the crossroads. The current walls were built afterward—they learned their lesson.

**The Gates:**
Each of the five gates has the same design: double portcullis, outer courtyard (a kill zone), inner gates. But each is named and decorated differently:

- **North Gate (Ironward):** Decorated with mountain symbols, handles traffic from Kaer Thandros
- **South Gate (Sunward):** Desert motifs, traffic from Sundara
- **East Gate (Seaward):** Maritime themes, traffic from Goldreach
- **West Gate (Forestward):** Woodland carvings, traffic from Silverpine
- **Center Gate (The Crown):** Finest decoration, ceremonial entrance

Guards at each gate check cargo (looking for contraband and assessing tariffs). The checking is thorough but professional. They've seen everything—be honest, pay your tariffs, move on.

### The Merchant District (The Hub)

**Entering:**
The five roads meet in a massive circular plaza—The Grand Circle—500 feet in diameter, paved with colored stones forming a compass rose pattern. This is the heart of Crossvale.

The plaza is controlled chaos. Merchants have stalls arranged in concentric circles. Goods from across Tirvandor are here: spices from the south, furs from the north, crafts from the east, lumber from the west. The noise is incredible—hundreds of merchants shouting, buyers haggling, goods being loaded and unloaded, animals protesting, street performers competing for attention.

The smell is everything at once: spices, leather, unwashed bodies, cooking food, animal dung, perfume, wood smoke. It's overwhelming until you adjust.

**Architecture - Merchant Quarter:**
- **Ground Floor:** Wide fronts open to the street, display windows, heavy shutters (closed and locked at night)
- **Materials:** Mixed—stone first floor (fire safety), timber or brick upper floors, tile roofs
- **Second Floor:** Business offices, counting houses, storage
- **Third Floor:** Merchant family living quarters
- **Fourth Floor:** Servant quarters, additional storage
- **Decoration:** Wealthy merchants display wealth—carved facades, painted plasterwork, guild symbols in iron


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**Typical Street Scene - The Counting Quarter:**

You're on Silver Street—one of the primary merchant streets radiating from The Grand Circle. It's 40 feet wide with raised sidewalks on both sides (keeps pedestrians separate from wagon traffic).

The buildings here are substantial—four stories, well-maintained, decorated but not gaudy. Ground floors are offices and showrooms. Large windows with glass panes display expensive goods. Signs hang above doors—carved wood with gold leaf, showing the merchant house name and symbol.

During business hours (dawn to dusk), the street is busy. Clerks run between offices carrying ledgers. Merchants meet to negotiate deals. Money changers operate from small shops (heavily guarded). Porters move goods. Everything is business.

The people here dress well—merchants in quality clothing (showing status but not flaunting), clerks in neat but simple tunics, guards in house colors (every major merchant employs guards).

**The Marketplace - The Grand Circle:**

The center of everything. During market hours (dawn to dusk, every day), it's packed.

The outer ring (closest to the street) is food vendors: bread, meat, vegetables, prepared food. The smell is incredible. Customers buy lunch, dinner, snacks. Street performers work this ring—musicians, jugglers, storytellers.

The middle ring is specialty goods: cloth merchants, leather workers, tool sellers, pottery, basketry. Quality goods at fair prices. Customers browse, compare, haggle.

The inner ring (closest to the center) is luxury goods: jewelry, fine cloth, exotic spices, rare books, art. Higher prices, wealthier customers, more selective vendors.

The very center is the Burgher's Platform—a raised stone dais where the city council makes announcements, where public declarations are read, where judgments are pronounced.

**Random Detail Table - Crossvale Market:**
Roll d20:
1. Price war between competing merchants, undercutting each other loudly
2. Exotic goods from distant lands drawing crowd, strange smells
3. Street magician performing tricks, pickpocket working the audience
4. Market inspector checking weights, merchant caught cheating
5. Caravan arriving, travel-stained merchants unloading goods
6. Street preacher warning about greed, mostly ignored by merchants
7. Noble's servant shopping with list, vendors competing for attention
8. Children running errands for families, dodging through crowds
9. Foreign merchant with language barrier, using gestures to haggle
10. Rival merchant houses having shouting match over territory
11. Street artist doing quick portraits, long line of customers
12. Exotic animal being led through, crowd parting nervously
13. Wedding party shopping for celebration, merchants offering discounts
14. Delivery going wrong, crate breaks, goods spilling everywhere
15. Insurance assessor examining damaged goods, merchant arguing
16. Guild representative collecting dues, ledger in hand
17. Tourist obviously lost, locals giving contradictory directions
18. Musician with trained monkey, collecting coins in cup
19. News crier selling broadsides, shouting headlines
20. Merchant celebrating successful deal, buying drinks for crowd

### The Warehouse District

**Located:** South and west of center, closer to walls

**Purpose:** Storage. Crossvale is a distribution hub—goods arrive, get stored, get redistributed. The warehouses make it work.

**Architecture - Warehouses:**
- **Size:** Massive—some 200 feet long, 100 feet wide, 40 feet high
- **Materials:** Stone first floor (fire safety, security), timber upper floor (lighter load)
- **Doors:** Huge double doors, reinforced, multiple locks
- **Windows:** Minimal, high up (security)
- **Special Features:** Loading docks, pulley systems for moving goods, night guards

**Street Scene - Warehouse Quarter:**

It's quieter here than the market. The streets are wider (need room for wagons). The buildings are functional, not decorative.

During the day, it's active—wagons loading and unloading, porters moving goods, clerks checking inventories, guards watching everything. The work is physical and constant.

At night, it's locked down tight. The guards don't sleep—warehouse fires and thieves are constant threats. They patrol in pairs, carrying lanterns, watching shadows.

### The Guildhalls

**Located:** North of Grand Circle, prestigious district

**Purpose:** Each major trade guild has a hall here—their headquarters, meeting place, showcase.

**Architecture - Guildhalls:**
These are statements of power and wealth. Each guild tries to outdo the others.

- **Merchant's League:** Largest hall, four stories, marble façade, gold leaf decorations
- **Wagoneer's Guild:** Massive stable attached, vehicle displays
- **Banker's Guild:** Fortified like a castle, vaults deep underground
- **Innkeeper's Guild:** Operates as demonstration inn, finest example of hospitality
- **Artisan's Guild:** Each craft has their best work displayed outside


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**Social Structure:**

Crossvale runs on money and connections. Status comes from wealth, guild membership, and political influence. The burgher council (wealthy merchants) effectively rules. The nominal lord is a figurehead.

**What Crossvale Offers:**
- **Trading:** Anything can be bought or sold here
- **Information:** Merchants know everything traveling the roads
- **Banking:** Major banking houses with international connections
- **Lodging:** From flophouses to luxury inns
- **Contracts:** Caravan guard work, trading opportunities
- **Contacts:** Everyone passes through eventually

**Why Players Visit:**
- Trading goods or acquiring supplies
- Following trade routes to investigate something
- Meeting contacts (everyone stops in Crossvale)
- Guarding caravans for income
- Investigating trade-related crimes (smuggling, fraud)

---

## Ashgate - City of Fire and Stone

**Population:** ~12,000 (55% human, 20% dwarf, 15% genasi-fire, 10% other)

### Approaching from the West

**5 Miles Out:**
The landscape changes gradually. Vegetation thins. The ground becomes rocky, volcanic soil. The air tastes of sulfur. You see steam vents in the distance—white plumes rising from the earth.

The temperature rises. Even in winter, it's warm here. In summer, it's oppressive. Travelers shed layers, drink more water, move slower.

**2 Miles Out:**
The volcanic region becomes obvious. Black stone everywhere—obsidian outcroppings catching sunlight, basalt cliffs, pumice fields. The ground is cracked in places, revealing red-orange glow beneath (lava flows, barely cooled).

Ash drifts in the air—not thick enough to choke, but present. It coats everything. Buildings in the distance look black and gray. Your skin feels gritty.

Ashgate itself appears—a city of black stone against gray ash. The walls are obsidian—not carved, but built from massive blocks of volcanic glass. The effect is alien, beautiful, and intimidating.

**The Gates:**
The gates are iron—heavy black iron that doesn't corrode in the volcanic atmosphere. They're decorated with flame motifs—not painted, but forged into the metal itself, glowing faintly red (enchanted to be always warm, never hot enough to burn).

Guards wear lightweight armor—full plate is too hot here. They favor chain mail with cloth underneath, face scarves against ash, goggles against volcanic glare. Their tabard shows a black tower with red flames—the city symbol.

### The Outer City - The Ashward

**Entering:**
Through the gates is a courtyard—but it's not paved with stone. It's natural volcanic rock, smooth where lava flowed and cooled. The surface is uneven, wavy, frozen mid-flow. In places, you can see where lava filled in patterns, creating natural art.

The smell is distinct—sulfur (not overwhelming, you adjust), hot stone, charcoal, and underneath it, the metallic tang of worked obsidian.

The sound is unique—volcanic glass has a different resonance when struck. The whole city has a crystalline quality to its acoustics. Voices carry strangely. Footsteps on obsidian sidewalks make a different sound than stone.

**Architecture - Ashward:**
- **Materials:** Obsidian blocks (lower wealth), basalt stone (middle class), pumice brick (lighter, upper stories)
- **Roofs:** Fired clay tiles (everything else would warp in heat), steep pitch (ash slides off)
- **Windows:** Small (keep heat out during day), shuttered, no glass (too hot—ash cloth instead)
- **Doors:** Wood with metal fittings (wrapped in cloth during ash storms)
- **Special:** Every building has ash catchers (roof features to collect and divert ash)
- **Color:** Black, gray, red—natural volcanic palette

**Typical Street Scene - Glass Market:**

You're on Obsidian Way—the main artisan street. It's 30 feet wide, paved with smooth basalt. Ash drifts down constantly—street sweepers work all day with brooms made of wire (stiff enough to move ash, flexible enough not to scratch stone).

Both sides are lined with workshops—obsidian crafters primarily. The street is famous for volcanic glass work: weapons, tools, jewelry, art pieces. Ashgate obsidian is renowned across Tirvandor—sharper than steel, beautiful, and magically receptive.

Each workshop is open-fronted during work hours. Inside, you see the process: raw obsidian chunks being examined (clarity, color, flaws), master crafters striking stone with precision (one wrong blow shatters everything), apprentices polishing finished pieces (bringing out the glass-like shine).

The sound is distinctive—obsidian being struck makes a high, clear ring. Different tones mean different densities. Master crafters can hear flaws. The street creates a strange music—dozens of workshops, each striking at different rhythms.

Between workshops are essential services: a stone merchant (selling raw obsidian), a tool maker (crafting the specialized chisels for volcanic glass), a water seller (essential—you dehydrate fast here), an ash filter maker (protective masks).

The people move deliberately—heat makes you slow, conserve energy. No one runs unless emergency. Conversation is quiet—loud talking wastes water (you breathe more).

**Random Detail Table - Ashgate Streets:**
Roll d20:
1. Ash storm blowing in, everyone taking cover, shuttering windows
2. Obsidian crafter accidentally shatters valuable piece, cursing loudly
3. Water seller ringing bell, people buying to rehydrate
4. Fire genasi performing flame tricks for crowds, unbothered by heat
5. Steam vent suddenly venting, people jumping back, regular occurrence
6. Street sweeper clearing ash, endless battle against nature
7. Tourist fainting from heat, locals helping to shade, giving water
8. Lava flow visible in distance, glowing orange against black rock
9. Volcanic tremor, buildings shaking, people unconcerned (normal here)
10. Apprentice running errands, carrying wrapped obsidian carefully
11. Fire cult procession, members in red robes, chanting
12. Obsidian merchant examining stones for quality, rejecting flawed ones
13. Children playing game with obsidian shards, surprisingly safe
14. Street vendor selling ash-proof food, heavily covered
15. Armorer fitting obsidian blade to weapon hilt, precise work
16. Scholar studying volcanic patterns, taking notes
17. Guard checking newcomers for heat tolerance, warning unprepared
18. Healer treating heat exhaustion, common ailment
19. News about volcanic activity, people discussing calmly
20. Phoenix sighting (rare), everyone stopping to watch, good omen


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

### The Hot Springs District

**Located:** Northeast section, built around natural hot springs

**The Springs:**
A network of natural hot springs—water heated by underground lava flows, emerging at temperatures from pleasantly warm to scalding. The springs are terraced, channeled, and built around. This district is the social center of Ashgate.

**Architecture - Springs District:**
Buildings here are built for bathing culture. Open courtyards, pools visible from streets, steam vents used for heating. Wealthier establishments have private pools. Public baths serve everyone.

**Public Bathhouse Scene:**

The largest bathhouse—The Eternal Flame—serves hundreds daily. It's built in tiers:

**Entry Level:** You pay admission (reasonable—bathing is necessity here, not luxury). You're given a cloth, soap, locker key. The air is thick with steam.

**Cold Pool:** First stop—cold water (actually just cool by local standards). Washes off ash, sweat, daily grime. It's crowded, social, noisy.

**Warm Pool:** Larger, multiple temperatures depending on which spring feeds each section. This is where people linger—talking, relaxing, conducting business. Ashgate's social life happens in bathhouses.

**Hot Pool:** Smaller, truly hot, therapeutic. Helps with volcanic ash lung, muscle aches from heat, joint pain. Only used for short periods.

**Steam Rooms:** Natural steam from volcanic vents. Good for breathing, skin, general health. Popular with crafters whose lungs suffer from obsidian dust.

**Social Life:**
Bathhouses are where deals get made, alliances form, gossip spreads, politics happen. Everyone bathes daily (necessity in the ash and heat). You see everyone—rich and poor, powerful and weak—naked in the same water. It creates a unique social dynamic.

### The Ashwarden Fortress

**Located:** Built into volcanic caldera wall, overlooking the city

**Purpose:** Headquarters of the Ashwardens—the organization that monitors volcanic activity, predicts eruptions, manages lava flows, and protects the city from fire-based threats.

**The Fortress:**
Black obsidian walls, red-glowing enchantments, constantly warm to the touch. The Ashwardens work day and night—monitoring deep earth tremors, watching lava flow patterns, maintaining the city's fire wards.

Inside: A mix of military post, research facility, and fire temple. Walls covered with maps showing lava tubes, historical eruption patterns, current threat assessments. Central chamber has a massive obsidian scrying orb—shows real-time volcanic activity beneath the city.

**The Wardens:**
A mix of druids, rangers, fire genasi, and specialized mages. They wear black robes with red flames. The populace treats them with respect bordering on reverence—the Wardens keep the city safe from the volcano's wrath.

### The Glass Bazaar

**Located:** Central market district

**What's Special:**
This marketplace specializes in volcanic glass goods. Everything obsidian—weapons, tools, jewelry, art, magical focuses, even architectural elements.

**The Experience:**
Walking through is like being in a museum. Display cases show masterwork pieces—obsidian daggers with edges sharper than razors, jewelry that catches light like captured fire, sculptures of volcanic glass in impossible detail.

The prices range from affordable (simple tools) to astronomical (masterwork weapons with magical properties). Wealthy collectors come from across Tirvandor to shop here.

**What Ashgate Offers:**
- **Obsidian Goods:** Best volcanic glass work in the world
- **Hot Springs:** Therapeutic bathing, social center
- **Fire Magic:** Fire genasi community, fire-based spell research
- **Volcanic Tours:** Guided expeditions (dangerous but lucrative)
- **Heat Resistance Training:** Learning to survive in volcanic environments

**Why Players Visit:**
- Buying obsidian weapons (masterwork quality)
- Researching fire magic or volcanic phenomena
- Investigating fire cult activity
- Seeking Ashwarden aid against fire threats
- Following rumors of phoenix sightings

---


\page

# Aethoria Cities - Detailed Walkthroughs

## Aethermere - The Starlight Capital

**Population:** ~42,000 (60% elf, 20% half-elf, 10% human, 10% other)

### Approaching from the South Road


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**5 Miles Out:**
The forest changes. Trees become larger, older, more deliberate in arrangement. The canopy overhead creates a green cathedral. Sunlight filters through in shafts—almost theatrical. The air smells of flowers, clean water, and something indefinable—magic perhaps, or just the scent of an ancient forest undisturbed.

You notice the quiet first. Not silence—birds sing, leaves rustle, water flows—but an absence of harsh sounds. No axes chopping, no construction noise, no urban cacophony. The forest has its own rhythm.

**2 Miles Out:**
The path becomes more deliberate. It's not paved, but it's clearly maintained—roots won't trip you, branches won't strike your face, the way is clear without being obviously cleared. Natural but guided.

You see the first outer buildings—but "buildings" is the wrong word. Structures grown as much as built. Trees shaped while living, branches woven into walls, living wood bent and trained into architecture. The effect is organic, beautiful, and slightly unsettling if you're from Thaldros.

**1 Mile Out:**
Lake Aether comes into view through the trees. It's massive—you can't see the far shore. The water is impossibly clear, impossibly still, reflecting the sky perfectly. In daylight, it's mirror-bright. At night (they say), it reflects stars that aren't in the actual sky.

The city rises from the lakeshore—but it doesn't tower. Aethermere spreads horizontally, growing into the forest rather than above it. Buildings flow between trees. Streets curve naturally. Nothing is straight-lined or rigid.

**Entering:**
There are no gates. No walls. No checkpoints. The transition from forest to city is gradual. You're walking through woods, then you're walking past dwellings, then you're in the city proper. You didn't notice when exactly it changed.

This openness is deliberate—Aethermere doesn't fear invasion. The elves have confidence (arrogance?) that they could defend without walls if needed. Or maybe they don't expect attack. Or maybe the forest itself is defense.

### The Lakeshore District - Aether's Edge

**The Approach:**
You emerge from tree-shaded paths to sudden openness—the lakeshore. The transition is dramatic. From forest shadow to lake-reflected light. Your eyes need a moment to adjust.

**The Docks:**
Not docks like Goldreach—no heavy piers, no industrial loading. These are elegant wooden platforms extending into the water, seemingly floating. Boats are moored—graceful sailboats, narrow canoes, magical constructs that move without visible propulsion.

The docks serve transportation more than trade—Aethermere imports little. The boats ferry people across the lake, to offshore islands, to lakeside settlements.

**Architecture - Lakeshore:**
- **Materials:** Living wood (trees shaped while growing), white stone (imported, used for public buildings), glass (large windows, capturing lake light)
- **Design:** Curves, not straight lines. Buildings flow into surroundings
- **Roofs:** Living branches woven overhead, flowering vines (decorative and functional—shade, rain cover)
- **Windows:** Large, open, minimal barriers (climate is mild, threats are rare)
- **Decoration:** Natural—flowering plants, water features, crystal ornaments catching light

**Typical Scene - Lakeshore Promenade:**

You're walking along the Starwalk—a broad path following the lakeshore. It's 50 feet wide, paved with smooth white stone that seems to glow faintly in moonlight (enchanted, probably).

On your left—the lake. So clear you can see fish swimming, underwater plants waving. The surface reflects everything—clouds, buildings, passersby. Children play at the water's edge. Artists sit painting the view. Couples walk hand in hand.

On your right—the city. Buildings rise organically from the ground—grown as much as built. Ground floors are open to the air—shops, cafes, workshops without walls, just roofs and floor. Everything flows together.

People move without hurry. There's a graceful quality to movement here—elves naturally glide, and others adapt to the pace. No one shouts. Conversations are quiet. Laughter is musical.

The smell is flowers—always flowers. Jasmine, honeysuckle, roses, others you don't recognize. The scent isn't overwhelming—it's natural, present without dominating.

**Random Detail Table - Aethermere Streets:**
Roll d20:
1. Elf children releasing floating lanterns onto lake, hundreds drifting
2. Street musician playing crystal chimes, hauntingly beautiful melody
3. Scholar debating philosophy with students, sitting in circle under trees
4. Artist painting watercolor of lake, crowd watching respectfully
5. Swan gliding near shore, definitely more intelligent than normal swans
6. Couple having wedding ceremony at water's edge, small gathering
7. Elderly elf feeding fish, hundreds swarming near dock
8. Merchant from Thaldros looking uncomfortable, out of place
9. Magic lesson in progress, students practicing illusions
10. Poet reciting verses, small audience listening intently
11. Water sprite appearing briefly, playing with children, vanishing
12. Moon elf performing nighttime blessing even during day
13. Craftsman demonstrating woodshaping, bending branch without tools
14. Healer tending injured bird, channeling magic gently
15. Scout returning from patrol, reporting to elder calmly
16. Stargazer setting up equipment despite daylight, preparing for evening
17. Dancer practicing by water, movements reflected in surface
18. Archery practice, targets floating on lake, incredible precision
19. Group meditation, dozen elves in perfect stillness
20. Fey creature visiting from Feywild, casually shopping

### The University - The Starlit Towers

**Located:** Northern section, built on slight elevation overlooking lake

**The Campus:**
Not enclosed—buildings spread through ancient grove. Each building serves different school of study. Paths wind between them, crossing bridges over streams, passing meditation gardens.

**The Buildings:**
Each school of magic has its tower—though "tower" is misleading. These structures rise only 4-5 stories but feel taller due to proportions. They're grown from ancient trees, wood shaped into architecture, with crystal additions capturing and refracting light.


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

- **Evocation Tower:** Scarred from practice—scorch marks, frost damage, acid stains. Constantly repaired. Most popular among younger students.
- **Divination Observatory:** Top floor is all glass—crystal dome capturing starlight even during day. Telescopes, scrying focuses, star charts everywhere.
- **Illusion Hall:** Seems to move when not directly observed. Architecture shifts subtly. Popular with artists and entertainers.
- **Enchantment Spire:** Beautiful, welcoming, draws you in. Decorated with crystals that whisper suggestions. Teaching through demonstration.
- **Necromancy Grove:** Set apart, darker trees, quieter. Not forbidden—elves accept death as part of cycle—but respected, approached seriously.
- **Library of Infinite Pages:** Largest building—five stories, seemingly larger inside than outside. Contains knowledge collected over three millennia.

**The Library:**

Worth special mention. This is one of the great repositories of knowledge in Tirvandor.

**Entry Hall:** Enormous chamber, soaring ceiling, natural light filtering through crystal skylights. The smell is old paper, leather bindings, preserved knowledge.

**The Stacks:** Stretch in all directions. The organization system is complex—not alphabetical or by subject alone, but by conceptual connections. Elves understand it intuitively. Others need guides.

**Special Collections:** Restricted sections—ancient texts, dangerous knowledge, prophecies, forbidden magic. Access requires permission, often denied.

**Reading Rooms:** Scattered throughout—quiet spaces with comfortable chairs, natural light, small tables. Scholars spend days here, lost in research.

**The Keepers:** Librarians here are more than organizers—they're guardians of knowledge, helping researchers find connections between texts, guiding study, occasionally denying access to things they deem unready for discovery.

### The Artisan Quarter - Silverleaf District

**Located:** Eastern section, closer to Silverwood forest proper

**Specialization:** Crafting using living materials—woodshaping, crystal growing, natural dye work, metal singing (working metal with magic rather than force).

**Architecture - Artisan Quarter:**
Workshops are gardens as much as buildings. Craftsmen work outdoors when possible. Tools are minimal—hands, magic, patience matter more than implements.

**Typical Workshop - Master Woodshaper:**

You enter a grove that's also a workshop. Trees grow in specific patterns—deliberately shaped over decades. The master works at the center, hands on a young sapling, shaping it through magic and will.

Around the grove—finished pieces. Furniture that grew as single pieces (no joints, no weak points). Bows made from still-living wood (stronger, more flexible). Musical instruments with grain patterns that enhance sound.

The work is slow—shaping living wood takes time. Months or years for a single piece. But the results are unmatched—beautiful, strong, perfectly suited to purpose.

**The Crystal Garden:**

Special location—where crystal artisans grow magical crystals for wands, focuses, jewelry.

The garden is a cave—natural formation—with quartz veins in the walls. Artisans introduce seeds (tiny crystal fragments) and use magic to encourage growth. The crystals grow slowly, forming specific shapes based on magical guidance.

The result—crystals with unique properties. Some enhance divination. Some store magic efficiently. Some sing when sunlight touches them. Each is unique.

### The Council Grove

**Located:** Geographic center of city

**Purpose:** Where the Council of Elders meets, makes decisions, resolves disputes, announces changes.

**The Grove:**
Ancient oaks—some over 2000 years old—arranged in circle around clear space. The oldest, largest oak stands at north—the Wisdom Tree, said to house the spirit of Aethermere's founder.

When Council meets, they sit on roots of the circle trees—no chairs, no formal furniture. The symbolism—they're rooted in the land, part of the forest, grounded in tradition.

**Council Meetings:**
Open to all—anyone can attend, witness, petition. But speaking requires recognition—you must be called upon. The process is formal without being rigid.

Decisions are made by consensus, not vote. Discussion continues until agreement emerges. This can take hours. Days. Weeks for important matters. The elves have patience.

**What Aethermere Offers:**
- **Education:** Finest magical education in Tirvandor
- **Healing:** Elven healers using natural magic
- **Crafting:** Masterwork items using living materials
- **Knowledge:** Library access (if you can navigate the system)
- **Meditation:** Quiet spaces for contemplation
- **Stargazing:** Clearest views of night sky

**Why Players Visit:**
- Researching magical topics
- Learning specific spells or traditions
- Commissioning elven-crafted items
- Seeking elven aid for nature-based problems
- Following prophecies or divinations
- Investigating Feywild connections

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

## Starfall - The Sacred City

**Population:** ~25,000 (55% elf, 25% half-elf, 15% human, 5% other)

### Approaching from Any Direction

**10 Miles Out:**
The terrain rises. You're climbing into foothills, then actual mountains. The forest here is different from Silverwood—more rugged, windswept, ancient.

At night (and Starfall is best approached at night), you see the lights. Not city lights—starlight. Focused, concentrated, brighter than it should be. The city glows with captured starlight.

**5 Miles Out:**
The mountains form a bowl—natural amphitheater facing the sky. Starfall sits at the bottom of this bowl, protected from wind, open to heaven. The positioning is perfect for astronomical observation—minimal light pollution, clear air, unobstructed view.

**2 Miles Out:**
The road becomes steeper. You're breathing hard. The air is thin—high altitude. But it's incredibly clear. On cloudless nights, you can see thousands of stars, the Milky Way like a river of light across the sky.

Starfall appears below—a city built in concentric circles, each ring lower than the last, descending into the valley floor. From above, it looks like a spiral galaxy reflected on earth.

**Entering:**
No walls—none needed at this altitude, this remote. The outer ring is residential—simple dwellings for support staff, families of scholars, visiting researchers.

The inner rings—progressively more important. Each ring requires greater access. The innermost ring—the Obserwatory Circle—is restricted to astronomers, divination specialists, those who read the stars.

### The Observatory Circle

**Located:** Valley floor, lowest and most protected point

**Architecture:**
Domes. Dozens of them. Crystal and metal, designed to open to the sky, to track celestial movements, to capture starlight for study.

The largest—the Grand Observatory—rises 100 feet high. Its dome is pure crystal, enchanted to be transparent when needed, opaque for protection, and magnifying for enhanced viewing.

**Inside the Grand Observatory:**

**Entry Level:** Maps. Walls covered with star charts—historical observations going back 2000 years. Tracking planetary movements, comet appearances, celestial events.

**Second Level:** Calculation rooms. Mathematicians working on orbital mechanics, predicting eclipses, calculating positions. The work is complex—blackboards covered with equations, arguments about degree measurements.

**Third Level:** Libraries. Books on astronomy, divination, celestial magic, prophecy. Restricted—not forbidden, but requires explanation of research purpose.

**Fourth Level:** The Observation Deck. The dome opens above. Massive telescope—crystal lenses enchanted to gather light, resolve detail impossible for normal eyes. Astronomers work in rotation—different specialties need different conditions.

**The Telescope:**
Not just for observation—it's a divination tool. By observing star patterns, alignments, celestial movements, trained diviners can predict events, interpret omens, understand cosmic patterns affecting the material plane.

The telescope is so sensitive that it can see things that shouldn't be visible—distortions in space, brief glimpses of other planes, anomalies that hint at cosmic events beyond mortal understanding.

### The Stargazer's District

**Located:** Second ring from center

**Population:** Scholars, diviners, astronomers, mathematicians, philosophers

**The Buildings:**
Designed around astronomy. Flat roofs for personal telescopes. Windows facing optimal directions. Dark interiors (light ruins night vision).

**Typical Scene - Evening:**

Dusk is sacred time in Starfall. As sun sets, the city comes alive.

Rooftops fill with people—setting up telescopes, checking star charts, discussing what to observe tonight. The excitement is palpable. Every clear night is opportunity—to see something new, confirm a theory, witness a predicted event.

As darkness deepens, the city goes quiet. Sound disturbs observation. Lights are dimmed, shielded. Everyone respects the work happening above.

Through the night, you hear occasional excitement—a discovery, a successful observation, a rare event. But mostly—quiet focus. Hundreds of eyes watching the sky.

Before dawn—activity increases. Last observations before sunrise. Checking one more thing. Recording final notes. Then—sleep. Astronomers work nocturnal schedules.

**The Starlight Library:**

Special collection—prophecies, divination records, cosmic event predictions.

The library is dark—deliberately. Eyes need to adjust from bright reading light to dark sky. The books are written in luminous ink—visible in dim light, won't ruin night vision.

The prophecies here are organized by time period, by diviner, by certainty level (some are clear, others ambiguous, some proven wrong). Researchers study patterns—how prophecies manifest, what signs precede major events.

### The Celestial Temple

**Located:** Third ring


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**Dedicated:** To Celestia, goddess of stars and fate

**The Temple:**
Open to sky—no roof, just pillars supporting arched pathways around central courtyard. The floor is polished black stone, reflecting stars perfectly when clean.

The temple serves as both worship site and divination tool. Priests read patterns in how starlight falls, how reflections align, how shadows form during celestial events.

**Worship Services:**
Held at specific astronomical events—new moons, full moons, planetary alignments, meteor showers, eclipses. The timing is precise—services begin when celestial events occur, not at convenient times.

The worship is quiet—meditation, observation, contemplation. Celestia's followers believe meaning emerges from watching, understanding patterns, accepting fate while working to comprehend it.

### The Prophecy Archive

**Located:** Fourth ring, heavily restricted

**Purpose:** Recording and studying prophecies, visions, divinations

**Security:** Not guarded—elves don't lock things typically—but access requires Council permission. The prophecies here are powerful, potentially dangerous, often misunderstood.

**Contents:**
- Major prophecies affecting Tirvandor's future
- Historical prophecies (to study how they manifested)
- Failed prophecies (important—understanding when divination was wrong helps interpret current visions)
- Sealed prophecies (deemed too dangerous or unclear to release)

**The Work:**
Scholars here study prophecy as science and art. They cross-reference visions from multiple sources, look for patterns, attempt to translate symbolic language into actionable understanding.

The challenge—prophecy is rarely literal. Symbols, metaphors, alternate interpretations all matter. A prophecy saying "the mountain falls" might mean: an actual mountain, political power represented by mountain, personal strength, metaphorical stability—who knows?

**What Starfall Offers:**
- **Prophecy:** Divination services (expensive, not always clear)
- **Star Reading:** Personal fate interpretation
- **Astronomical Knowledge:** Best star charts, planetary tables
- **Training:** Learning divination, scrying, far-seeing
- **Sanctuary:** Quiet retreat for contemplation

**Why Players Visit:**
- Seeking specific prophecy or divination
- Researching celestial events
- Learning about fate, destiny, predetermined events
- Following prophecy clues
- Witnessing rare astronomical event

- Crossvale (Major City - Trade Hub)
- Ashgate (Major City - Volcanic Region)
- Aethermere (Major City - Aethoria Capital)

## Crystalbrook - The Jewel of the Lakes

**Population:** ~30,000 (50% elf, 20% human, 15% halfling, 10% gnome, 5% other)

### Approaching from the West

**3 Miles Out:**
The landscape is lakes. Dozens of them—connected by streams, separated by narrow land bridges, creating a maze of water and shore. The water is crystalline—you can see to the bottom even in deep sections. The clarity is supernatural.

The roads here are raised causeways—built up above water level, allowing passage between lakes. During spring floods, some causeways disappear underwater—you need boats.

**1 Mile Out:**
Crystalbrook appears—but it's not what you expect. The city isn't built on one shore—it's spread across a dozen islands, connected by bridges, each island a distinct district.

The water between islands is busy—boats everywhere. Canoes, sailboats, barges, rafts. This is a water city. Many residents never touch dry land.

The architecture is designed for reflections—white stone, crystal windows, mirrored surfaces. The entire city reflects in the water, creating a doubled effect. From a distance, you can't tell which is city and which is reflection.

**Entering:**
There's no main gate—too many entry points. You arrive at one of the outer islands and proceed inward. Bridges connect everything. Some bridges are arched stone. Some are flat wooden platforms. Some are magical constructs—force bridges that appear when needed.

The first thing you notice—the sound. Water everywhere. Lapping against shores, flowing through channels, dripping from fountains, splashing from boats. The city has a constant water soundtrack.


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

The smell is clean—fresh water, aquatic plants, occasionally fish, but mostly just pure water scent.

### The Central Island - Crystal Heart

**Located:** Geographic center, largest island

**Purpose:** Government, commerce, major landmarks

**Architecture - Central Island:**
Buildings here are white marble and crystal. The marble is quarried from underwater deposits (special magic-resistant stone). The crystal comes from the lakes themselves—natural formations that grow in the clear water, harvested and shaped.

**The Grand Plaza:**

A massive circular plaza—500 feet across—paved with white marble that's been polished mirror-smooth. When wet (which is often), it reflects the sky perfectly. During rain, the plaza becomes a shallow mirror—people seem to walk on clouds.

Around the plaza—major buildings:

- **The Lake Palace:** Where the Council of Waters meets. Four stories, crystal walls, water flowing through interior channels. You can see fish swimming through the building.
- **The Crystal Exchange:** Trading house for gems, crystals, magical focuses. Heavily guarded. Crystals from Crystalbrook are valued across Tirvandor.
- **The Tidecaller's Guild:** Water magic specialists, engineers who manage the city's water systems, hydrologists.
- **The Harbor Master's Hall:** Coordinates boat traffic (essential—hundreds of boats daily).

**Typical Scene - Crystal Heart Plaza:**

Midday. The plaza is busy—market day. Merchants have set up stalls under canvas awnings (sun reflecting off water and white stone is intense).

The goods are water-focused: boats, fishing gear, water-resistant clothing, aquatic plants (medicinal and culinary), crystals and gems, mirrors, glass work, ice (harvested from northern lakes, transported in magical cold boxes).

Boats arrive constantly at the island's edges—merchants unloading goods, customers departing after purchases, water taxis carrying passengers between islands.

The people move differently here—they're conscious of water nearby. No running (easy to fall in). Careful footing on wet stone. Casual grace from lifelong practice.

Children play at water's edge—supervised. Falling in isn't dangerous (everyone swims), but parents watch anyway. Lifeguards are stationed at intervals—more for rescue from currents than drowning.

**Random Detail Table - Crystalbrook Streets:**
Roll d20:
1. Water taxi race between competitive boatmen, passengers holding on
2. Crystal merchant displaying gems in sunlight, spectacular rainbows
3. Child falling off dock, rescued immediately, parent scolding
4. Street vendor selling grilled fish, caught that morning
5. Water weird performing in fountain, gathering crowd
6. Boat collision, both drivers arguing about right-of-way
7. Storm approaching, everyone checking boat moorings, securing goods
8. Ice sculptor creating piece on hot day, racing melting
9. Diving competition at deep dock, excellent swimmers showing off
10. Aquatic elf visiting from underwater city, rare sight
11. Mirror seller demonstrating magical mirrors, showing distant places
12. Flood warning announced, people moving to higher islands
13. Romantic couple in boat, being serenaded by water genasi musician
14. Glass bottom boat offering tours, showcasing underwater ruins
15. Pearl diver emerging with find, crowd gathering to see
16. Water elemental moving through canal, people clearing path respectfully
17. Bridge temporarily failed, magic needs recharging, crowd waiting
18. Underwater window shop—viewing aquatic life below street level
19. Rain started, everyone continuing normally, unbothered
20. Crystal resonance event—all crystals humming same tone, magical

### The Bridges

**Essential Infrastructure:**
Crystalbrook has over 200 bridges. Some are famous, named, historic. Others are functional, numbered, maintained.

**Bridge Types:**

**Stone Arch Bridges:** Oldest, most solid. Built during city founding. White marble, carved decorations, built to last millennia. Wide enough for carts. These connect major islands.

**Floating Pontoon Bridges:** Temporary, movable. Used where permanent bridges aren't practical. Made from treated wood, enchanted for buoyancy. Can be disconnected and moved during floods.

**Crystal Bridges:** Magical constructs. Transparent, seemingly fragile, actually incredibly strong. Expensive to maintain. Used for prestigious locations.

**Force Bridges:** Pure magic. Appear when activated (command word or gesture), disappear when not needed. Used for private access, emergencies, or special events.

**Rope Bridges:** Narrow, swaying, unnerving. Connect smaller islands or provide shortcuts. Locals use them confidently. Visitors go slowly.

### The Waterways

**Not Just Background:**
The canals, channels, and lake passages are streets. Boat traffic is regulated like wagon traffic in other cities.

**Traffic Rules:**
- Larger boats have right-of-way
- Clockwise travel around islands
- No anchoring in main channels
- Speed limits in residential areas
- Horn signals for intentions (turning, stopping, passing)


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**Boat Types:**

**Water Taxis:** For hire. Flat-bottomed boats, room for 4-6 passengers. Driver stands at rear, uses single oar for propulsion. Fast, efficient, cheap.

**Cargo Barges:** Flat, wide, slow. Transport goods between islands. Pulled by swimming horses or propelled by poles.

**Personal Canoes:** Everyone owns one. Used like you'd use a horse elsewhere. Stored at docks by homes, businesses.

**Sailboats:** For longer trips across larger lakes. Elegant, fast when wind cooperates, slower than paddle boats in calm.

**Magical Craft:** Expensive. Self-propelled, no visible means of movement. Status symbols. Used by wealthy, government officials.

### The Underwater City

**Exists:** Below the surface, constructed by aquatic elves, accessible to those with water-breathing.

**What's There:**
- Markets for underwater resources (special corals, deep-lake plants, pearls)
- Residences for aquatic races
- Research stations studying deep lake ecology
- Magical laboratories (underwater environment useful for certain magic)
- Ancient ruins (city built on top of older civilization, partially submerged)

**Access:**
For surface dwellers—rental water-breathing magic (potions, temporary enchantments, paid breathing spells). Expensive but available.

**Why Go:**
Curiosity, unique shopping, underwater ruins exploration, research, trade with aquatic races.

### The Crystal Gardens

**Located:** Southern island cluster

**Purpose:** Growing magical crystals for commercial harvest

**The Gardens:**
Not gardens in normal sense. Underwater plots where crystals grow naturally, tended by crystal farmers who encourage specific growth patterns.

**The Process:**
1. Seed crystal planted in lake bed (specific depth, water composition matters)
2. Natural magic in water encourages growth
3. Crystal farmers monitor, adjust water conditions, use magic to guide formation
4. Growth takes years (small crystals) to decades (large formations)
5. Harvest carefully (breaking wrong way ruins crystal)
6. Polish, cut, enchant, sell

**The Quality:**
Crystalbrook crystals are famous. The water purity, magical saturation, and skillful cultivation produce crystals ideal for spell focusing, magical item construction, divination tools.

**What Crystalbrook Offers:**
- **Crystals and Gems:** Finest magical crystals in Tirvandor
- **Water Magic:** Schools teaching water-based spells
- **Boat Building:** Master craftsmen creating specialized watercraft
- **Mirror Magic:** Scrying, communication, illusion work using mirrors
- **Underwater Exploration:** Guided tours of submerged ruins
- **Fishing:** Some of best fishing in Tirvandor

**Why Players Visit:**
- Buying/commissioning magical crystals or focuses
- Researching water magic or aquatic phenomena
- Exploring underwater ruins
- Investigating crystal theft or smuggling
- Seeking aquatic allies or information
- Learning underwater combat or survival

---

## Wildgrove - The Jungle Heart

**Population:** ~20,000 (40% elf, 30% human, 20% halfling, 10% other)

### Approaching Through the Jungle

**10 Miles Out:**
The jungle is different here—wilder, older, more alive. The trees are enormous—some 300 feet tall, trunks 40 feet in diameter, roots that form walls. The canopy is so thick that ground level is in perpetual twilight.

The jungle is loud—birds calling, monkeys screaming, insects buzzing, unseen animals moving through undergrowth. It's overwhelming at first.

The heat is oppressive. 90+ degrees, 90% humidity. You sweat constantly. Clothing sticks to skin. Breathing feels thick.


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**5 Miles Out:**
The path becomes more deliberate—not cleared, but guided. Vines trimmed just enough for passage. Roots carved with steps. Markers showing the way (natural-looking but definitely placed).

You start seeing signs of habitation—carved symbols on trees (territory markers, warnings, directions), rope bridges overhead (connecting canopy levels), occasional treehouses in lower branches.

**2 Miles Out:**
The forest becomes cultivated without looking cultivated. The fruit trees are too regularly spaced to be natural. The useful plants are too common. The dangerous plants have been removed. But it all looks wild—deliberate invisibility.

Wildgrove itself isn't visible until you're almost there. The city is IN the forest—built into trees, under canopy, using living wood. It doesn't impose on jungle—it becomes part of jungle.

**Entering:**
No gates, no walls, no obvious boundary. You're in jungle, then you're passing dwellings, then you're in the city proper. The transition is gradual.

The city exists on multiple levels:
- **Ground Level:** Warehouses, workshops, water collection, gardens
- **Lower Canopy (20-50 feet):** Residential, markets, common spaces
- **Mid Canopy (50-100 feet):** Wealthier residences, government, special buildings
- **Upper Canopy (100+ feet):** Observatories, lookouts, sacred spaces

Moving between levels requires climbing—rope ladders, carved steps in trees, pulley platforms, trained animals (giant lizards with passenger saddles).

### The Ground Level - The Roots

**The Experience:**
Dim light filtered through leaves. Humidity intense. Temperature slightly cooler than above canopy. The smell is rich—earth, rot, flowers, spices, animals.

The ground is covered with root systems—massive roots from trees above, creating a maze. Buildings are built between roots, using them as walls, supports.

**Architecture - Ground Level:**
- **Materials:** Living wood (shaped roots), woven vines, treated leaves (waterproofing), bamboo-like plants
- **Purpose:** Storage, workshops requiring space, water collection cisterns
- **Design:** Natural—buildings blend into root structures
- **Windows:** Few—light is limited at ground level anyway

**Typical Scene - Root District:**

You're walking through a passage between two massive roots—they form natural walls 15 feet high. The path is packed earth, well-traveled, maintained.

Both sides have workshops built into root hollows—a carpenter using living wood (shaping, not cutting), a weaver working jungle fibers, a potter using clay from nearby riverbank, a leather worker processing hide.

The workshops are open-fronted—no doors, just awnings for rain protection. Everyone can see everyone working—communal, transparent.

Between workshops—cisterns collecting rainwater (essential—can't drink river water directly), food storage (elevated to prevent rot and pests), animal pens (small jungle pigs, birds, lizards for eggs/meat).

The noise is work sounds mixed with jungle sounds—hammering, sawing, weaving, plus bird calls, monkey chatter, insect buzz. Everything blends.

### The Lower Canopy - The Living Level

**Located:** 20-50 feet up

**Access:** Rope ladders (permanent, well-maintained), carved steps in tree trunks, pulley platforms (operated by hand or trained animals)

**The Experience:**
More light—not bright, but visible. Air movement (slight breeze). Temperature same but feels better with air flow.

This is where most residents live. Platforms built between trees, using branches as supports, creating a second ground level 30 feet up.

**Architecture - Lower Canopy:**
- **Platforms:** Woven from strong vines, treated for waterproofing, supported by living branches
- **Buildings:** Light construction—bamboo frames, leaf roofs, vine walls (privacy without weight)
- **Bridges:** Connect everything—platforms linked by rope bridges, wooden walkways, vine swings for the brave
- **Gardens:** Everywhere—planters, climbing plants, hanging gardens, using every surface

**Typical Scene - Residential Platform:**

You're standing on a platform 30 feet up—it's surprisingly solid, doesn't sway much. The platform is 200 feet long, 50 feet wide, with about 20 family dwellings.

Each dwelling is a simple structure—bamboo frame, leaf-thatch roof, woven vine walls. Inside (you can see through gaps)—hammocks for sleeping (beds retain too much heat), storage baskets hanging from ceiling, cooking area (small fire in clay pot, vented carefully), water barrel (filled daily from ground-level cisterns).

Between dwellings—communal spaces. Cooking areas (larger fires, shared use). Children's play areas (supervised constantly—falling is real danger). Meeting spaces (benches woven from vines, shaded areas for conversations).

The bridges connecting platforms are in constant use—people visiting neighbors, children running (despite parental warnings), workers heading to/from jobs, vendors carrying goods.

The air smells of cooking—spices, roasted meat, jungle fruits, something always cooking in communal areas.

**Random Detail Table - Wildgrove Platforms:**
Roll d20:
1. Child chasing monkey that stole food, both scrambling across bridges
2. Healer treating someone for jungle fever, using local herbs
3. Merchant unpacking exotic goods, crowd gathering to see
4. Platform sagging, workers reinforcing supports, concerned residents
5. Cooking competition, multiple fires going, judges tasting
6. Rope bridge frayed, being replaced, people taking longer route
7. Tropical storm approaching, everyone securing loose items
8. Snake in dwelling, expert calmly removing it, common occurrence
9. Birth celebration, entire platform gathered, singing, dancing
10. Tree surgeon treating sick support tree, serious concern
11. Water carrier delivering to upper platforms, using pulley system
12. Hunting party returning, displaying successful catch
13. Argument over platform space, elder mediating
14. Flying creature (bird? reptile?) swooping close, everyone ducking
15. Musician performing, using jungle instruments—hollow logs, shells, dried seeds
16. Child's first climb to lower canopy, family watching proudly
17. Visitor from Thaldros struggling with heat, locals helping
18. Emergency—fire on platform, everyone responding with water
19. Weaver repairing bridge, working while suspended
20. Night market setting up as sun sets, torches being lit


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

### The Mid Canopy - The Power Level

**Located:** 50-100 feet up

**Access:** Fewer connections—more controlled access, better-maintained paths

**Purpose:** Government, wealth, specialization

Buildings here are more substantial—still using living wood, but shaped over decades, grown into specific forms. More permanent, more impressive.

**The Great Tree:**

The city center—literally. An ancient tree, 400 years old, 250 feet tall, 50-foot diameter trunk. The trunk has been shaped into a hollow central column with spiral staircase carved inside.

Different levels serve different purposes:

**Base Level (ground):** Open courtyard, public gathering space
**Level 1 (30 feet):** Public services—permit office, census takers, public records
**Level 2 (50 feet):** Council chambers—where city governance happens
**Level 3 (70 feet):** Meeting rooms for factions, guild halls, negotiation spaces
**Level 4 (90 feet):** Archives—city history, important documents, protected
**Level 5 (110 feet):** Elder's quarters—private, rarely opened to public

**The Council Chambers:**

Worth describing. You climb the internal staircase (walls are living wood, slightly bioluminescent—provides light). At level 2, you emerge into a circular chamber 60 feet in diameter.

The walls are the tree itself—living wood curved into smooth surface. Windows (actually carefully shaped gaps) provide light and air. The floor is a single massive wooden disc—grown as part of tree, polished smooth over centuries.

Seating is simple—benches grown from the floor itself, arranged in circles. The council sits in the center circle. Advisors and observers sit in outer rings. Everyone can see everyone—transparency is cultural value.

The ceiling is 20 feet high—cathedral-like. Vines hang decoratively. Small birds nest in corners (considered good luck). The acoustics are perfect—everyone can be heard.

### The Upper Canopy - The Sacred Level

**Located:** 100+ feet up

**Access:** Restricted—requires permission, escort, or specific purpose

**The Experience:**
Above most foliage—more sunlight, cooler temperature (slight breeze at this height), incredible views (can see miles across jungle canopy).

This level is for observation, communication, spiritual practice.

**The Observatory Platforms:**

Multiple platforms at treetops—used for:
- **Weather Watching:** Predicting storms (critical in jungle)
- **Fire Spotting:** Jungle fires are catastrophic—early detection saves lives
- **Animal Migration:** Tracking flying creature patterns
- **Stars:** Astronomy (different tradition from Starfall's methods)
- **Communication:** Signal fires, mirror flashing, magical sending

**The Sacred Grove:**

Special location—cluster of ancient trees, platforms connecting them, used for spiritual practices.

The jungle elves follow nature-based traditions—honoring forest spirits, seasons, cycles of growth and decay. The upper canopy—closer to sky, further from earth—is considered threshold between worlds.

Rituals here involve:
- Sunrise/sunset observations
- Moon ceremonies (when visible through canopy)
- Plant growth blessings
- Animal migration blessings
- Weather prayers (for rain when needed, sun when needed)

Access is open for ceremonies, restricted otherwise—sacred spaces are respected.

### Jungle Markets

**Located:** Multiple locations, lower and mid canopy

**Specialization:** Jungle products

**What's For Sale:**
- **Food:** Tropical fruits, vegetables unknown outside jungle, spiced meats, insect protein (dried beetles, roasted grubs—actually quite good)
- **Medicine:** Jungle herbs (most effective healing plants grow here), poisons (if you know who to ask), antidotes (essential—jungle is dangerous)
- **Materials:** Exotic woods, jungle plant fibers, animal hides (ethically sourced—overhunting is taboo), feathers, bones
- **Live Animals:** Birds, monkeys, lizards, insects (some as pets, some as livestock, some as familiars)
- **Crafts:** Woven items, carved wood, jungle jewelry (seeds, shells, small bones)


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**The Experience:**
Markets are loud, colorful, overwhelming. Vendors call out in multiple languages. Goods are displayed creatively—hanging from vines, arranged on leaves, in woven baskets. Bargaining is expected—but fair. Cheating is remembered, reputation matters.

### What Wildgrove Offers:
- **Jungle Products:** Materials and goods unavailable elsewhere
- **Guides:** Experts for jungle travel (essential—jungle kills unprepared)
- **Healing:** Jungle medicine (different from temple healing, equally effective)
- **Animal Training:** Experts in training jungle creatures
- **Survival Training:** Learning jungle survival skills
- **Druid Traditions:** Nature magic, animal communication, plant growth

**Why Players Visit:**
- Seeking rare jungle materials
- Hiring guides for expedition
- Researching jungle creatures or plants
- Treating exotic diseases
- Following leads about jungle ruins
- Learning survival skills

---


\page

# Notable Towns - Detailed Scenes

## Deepforge - The Volcanic Smithies

**Population:** ~3,500 (80% dwarf, 20% other)
**Location:** Deep mountains, volcanic vents provide heat

### The Settlement:

Built in a caldera—extinct volcano crater, now stable. The lava has cooled but volcanic heat remains, channeled for forges.

**The Forges:**
Not coal-fired—these smithies use volcanic heat directly. Channels carved in rock direct heat from deep vents to forge chambers. Temperature control is precise—different vents, different temperatures.

**Why It Matters:**
Volcanic heat allows for special metalwork—higher temperatures, more precise control, unique alloys impossible elsewhere. Deepforge produces masterwork weapons, armor, specialty items.

**The Experience:**
Incredibly hot—even dwarves struggle. The air shimmers. Metal glows. The sound is constant—hammering, roaring heat, quenching steam.

**What Players Find:**
- Custom weapon/armor crafting (expensive, worth it)
- Rare metals (volcanic minerals with special properties)
- Master smiths (learning opportunities)
- Volcanic research (understanding fire magic, earth magic intersection)

### Random Encounter - Deepforge:
Roll d6:
1. Lava tube breached—emergency evacuation, forge cooling
2. Master smith accepts commission for legendary weapon
3. Earth tremor—minor, locals unconcerned, visitors nervous
4. New ore vein discovered—excited speculation about properties
5. Quenching accident—smith burned, healing needed
6. Competition between smiths—forging same item, judging quality

---

## Westmarch - The Frontier Outpost

**Population:** ~1,600 (70% human, 20% half-elf, 10% other)
**Location:** Western wilderness, last settlement before untamed lands

### The Outpost:

Small but fortified. Palisade walls (wooden—stone is too expensive here). Watch towers. Gates that actually close at night (unlike peaceful settlements).

**Architecture:**
Functional. No decoration. Buildings designed for defense—small windows, heavy doors, clear lines of sight, interior access between structures (don't need to go outside during attacks).

**The People:**
Rangers, scouts, hunters, trappers, adventurers using this as base. Everyone is armed. Everyone knows how to fight. This isn't because they're violent—it's because they need to be ready.

**What Threatens Them:**
- Bandits (using western wilderness as hideout)
- Monsters (unclaimed wilderness has things that eat people)
- Raiders (from even further west, beyond known settlements)
- Nature (harsh winters, dangerous animals, difficult terrain)


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**What Players Find:**
- Ranger training opportunities
- Wilderness survival experts
- Information about western threats
- Work (always need guards, scouts, hunters)
- Staging point for western expeditions

### Typical Scene - Westmarch:

Evening. Gates closing. Everyone inside. The walls are manned—four guards per wall section, rotating shifts.

The common hall is full—entire settlement gathers here evenings. It's warm (central fire), loud (everyone talking), smells of cooking (communal meal—rationing supplies).

Conversations are practical: weather, game movements, tracks found, strange occurrences, who's going where tomorrow. These people live on information—knowing things keeps you alive.

New arrivals (like players) are noted immediately. Westmarch's leader—a grizzled ranger named Kael Stormwatcher—will approach. Not hostile, but direct: "What's your business here? How long staying? What skills?" Not rude—just practical. He needs to know who's in his outpost.

---

## Moonwell - The Lunar Sanctuary

**Population:** ~2,800 (60% elf, 25% human, 15% other)
**Location:** Built around lunar well that glows with moonlight

### The Well:

Natural formation—spring that emerges from rock, but the water glows with silver light. Not metaphor—actual visible glow, bright enough to read by. The light intensifies during full moon, dims during new moon, but never goes completely dark.

**The Water:**
Sacred. Not drunk casually—used for rituals, healing, moon magic, divination. The druids here protect the well, manage access, prevent desecration.

**Architecture:**
Built in circles around the well. Inner circle—temple. Middle circle—druid dwellings. Outer circle—residents and visitors. Everything oriented toward the well—symbolic and practical (the light provides illumination at night).

**The Moon Druids:**
Specialized circle—work with lunar magic, cycles, tides (even far from ocean), growth patterns tied to moon phases. They're scholars and practitioners—studying moon's influence, using it for beneficial magic.

**What Players Find:**
- Lunar magic training
- Prophecy services (moon reveals hidden truths)
- Healing (moon water has properties)
- Astronomical knowledge (moon phases, celestial events)
- Peaceful sanctuary (violence near the well is forbidden—strictly enforced)

### Night Scene - Moonwell:

Full moon. The well is brightest—casting enough light to create shadows. The entire settlement glows silver.

The druids are performing ceremony—standing in circle around well, chanting in Sylvan, gestures synchronized. The water responds—glowing brighter, patterns forming on surface, images appearing (visions, prophecies, messages from moon goddess).

Visitors watch from respectful distance. Some are pilgrims—faithful seeking blessings. Some are scholars—studying lunar phenomena. Some are seekers—looking for answers only moon can provide.

The ceremony takes hours—druids are patient, methodical. No rushing sacred work. The air is charged—not dangerous, but potent. Magic feels close, accessible, present.

---

## Vineheart - The Jungle Traders

**Population:** ~2,700 (50% human, 30% halfling, 20% other)
**Location:** Jungle clearing, major trade stop for jungle resources

### The Settlement:

Built in a rare large clearing—unusual in jungle, probably artificial (cleared centuries ago, maintained since). The clearing is 500 feet across—enough space for buildings, markets, open areas.

The jungle presses close—you're always aware it's there, waiting. Vines grow fast—constant maintenance needed to keep them from reclaiming the clearing.

**Architecture:**
Elevated buildings—stilts raise floors 6 feet up (flood protection, pest protection, ventilation). Steep roofs (rain runoff). Open walls (air circulation—enclosed buildings are ovens).

**The Market:**
The reason Vineheart exists. This is where jungle resources get traded to merchants heading back to civilization.

**What's Traded:**
FROM JUNGLE (going out):
- Exotic woods, fruits, spices, medicines, animal products, crafts

TO JUNGLE (coming in):
- Metal tools, preserved foods, cloth, rope, glass, manufactured goods


{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{footnote TIRVANDOR WORLD GUIDE}}

\page

**The Traders:**
Professionals. They know jungle products—what's valuable, what's common, what's fake, what's illegal. They assess quality rapidly, offer fair prices (reputation matters—word spreads in jungle), arrange transport.

**What Players Find:**
- Jungle guides for hire
- Information about deeper jungle
- Unusual purchases (things not available in cities)
- Work (guards for trading caravans)
- Contacts with jungle dwellers

### Market Scene - Vineheart:

Morning. The market is setting up—merchants arranging goods, jungle harvesters bringing products from deep forest, traders examining inventory.

A jungle elf arrives with basket of rare orchids (valuable for perfume, medicine, alchemy). Three merchants bid—calling prices, showing currency. The elf is patient—knows value, won't be rushed. Eventually—agreement reached, orchids sold, elf departs with goods (not currency—barter preferred).

A young human couple—clearly new to jungle—try to buy machetes. The merchant asks questions: "Where heading? How long? What experience?" Not nosiness—responsibility. He won't sell inadequate equipment to people about to die. He recommends they hire guide, get training, start with shorter trips. They're offended at first, then realize he's right. They leave to find guide (merchant will point them to reputable ones).

An argument breaks out—trader claims wood quality isn't as promised. The seller is indignant—brings witnesses, shows documentation. The market elder arrives—examines wood, questions both parties, makes judgment. The wood IS as promised. The trader pays (and loses reputation for false claim).

---

## Using Street-Level Details: DM Advice

### Example Scene Flow:

**Setup:**
"You enter Kaer Thandros through the outer gates. The massive iron-bound doors tower 40 feet above you. The courtyard beyond is paved with perfectly fitted stones, worn smooth by centuries of boots. The air hits you immediately—coal smoke, worked iron, and the constant sound of hammering from dozens of forges."

**Player Response:**
"I want to find a smith to repair my sword."

**DM Response:**
"You're in the Outer City. Armorer's Row is the main street—you see forge glow spilling from every third or fourth doorway, hear the hammering echo constantly. The heat radiates 10 feet into the street. Master smiths' signs hang above doors—carved hammers, anvils, specific weapons. Which type of smith do you approach?"

**Improvisation:**
Player: "I look for someone who does blade work specifically."

DM: [Rolls on random detail table—gets result 16: "Dwarf forgemaster examining apprentice's work, shaking head disapprovingly"]

DM: "You spot a dwarf master smith outside a forge marked with a longsword sign. He's examining a blade his apprentice just finished, turning it in the light, shaking his head. 'The tang's too thin,' he's saying. 'Reforge it.' He notices you, looks at your sword, nods once. 'Blade needs work. I can see that from here. Come inside.'"

### The Goal:

Not to memorize everything. Not to describe everything. Just to give enough detail that players can see, hear, and feel the location. The street-level details are your toolkit—use what you need when you need it.

- **Major Cities (Fully Detailed):** 
  - Thaldros: Kaer Thandros, Silverpine, Goldreach, Sundara, Marshaven, Ironhold, Crossvale, Ashgate
  - Aethoria: Aethermere, Starfall, Crystalbrook, Wildgrove
- **Villages (Fully Detailed):** Oakenheart, Bogwallow, Frostholm
- **Notable Towns (Scene-Detailed):** Deepforge, Westmarch, Moonwell, Vineheart

- **DM implementation tips and example scene flows**

This document provides comprehensive immersive, street-level detail that DMs can use to bring locations to life during gameplay across all of Tirvandor's diverse regions.

# Tirvandor
## The Realm of Deep Magic

___

Tirvandor is a world divided—two great continents separated by the Sundering Sea, each representing different philosophies of civilization and magic. 

In the east lies **Thaldros**, a militaristic empire built on order and central authority. In the west sprawls **Aethoria**, a confederation of free cities valuing independence and democratic ideals.

But beneath the political tensions lies something older and more dangerous: the scars of the **Worldrend**, a cataclysm that tore the world apart three thousand years ago. Now, ancient powers stir, forgotten artifacts resurface, and the choices of a few heroes may determine whether history repeats itself.

___

**This World Guide contains:**

- Complete geography of both continents
- 3,000 years of history and timeline
- The full pantheon of gods
- Major factions and secret societies
- 18 noble houses with full genealogies
- 200+ detailed settlements
- Regional lore and adventure hooks
- Street-level descriptions for immersive play

___

*A campaign setting for the fifth edition of the world's greatest roleplaying game*
